# Engineering Mathematics IV: question papers in full

40 unique papers · 2010-11 to 2022-23 · Computer Engineering, Civil, Information Technology, Mechanical, Mechatronics, Electrical, Electronics and Telecommunication, Common (all branches)

Each paper: year, exam, semester, departments and programmes, then its full text as extracted. Scanned papers were read by OCR, so equations and symbols may contain recognition errors. Structured data: [`questions.jsonl`](questions.jsonl) (one record per question) and [`papers.jsonl`](papers.jsonl) (one record per paper). Index of files: [`README.md`](README.md).

The monthly income of 1000 workers follow normal distribution with mean of Rs.
3,000 and standard deviation of Rs. 500. Find

i) Number of workers having income more than Rs. 4,000.

ii) The lowest monthly income of the highest paid 50 workers.

Ina factory 40% workers are skilled. A group of five workers is randomly selected
from this factory. Find probability that group consists of i) All skilled workers ii) At
least two skilled workers.

A certain insurance company has 2000 policy holders. The chance that any one of them
will claim within a year iss Using appropriate probability distribution, find the

probability that during next year i) No one will claim , ii) Exactly THREE will claim.

A machine is expected to produce nails of length 7 cm. A random sample of 10 nails
was found to measure the following lengths: 7.2, 7.3, 7.1, 6.9, 6.8, 6.5, 6.9, 6.8, 7.1 and
7.2 cm. On the basis of this sample, what can be said about the reliability of the
machine at 5% level of significance?

Two operators working on a lathe machine produce shafts of different diameters. The
diameters of random samples of size 8 and 7 measured are given below:
Sample 1 | 19 17 15 21 16 18 16 14
Sample 2 | 15 14 15 19 15 18 16
Is the difference between the sample means significant at 5% level?

The mean breaking strength of the cables supplied by a manufacturer is 1800, with
standard deviation of 100. By a new technique in manufacturing process, it is claimed
that the breaking strength of the cable has increased. To test this claim, a sample of 50
cables is tested and it is found that the mean breaking strength is 1850. Can we support
the claim at 1% level of significance?

workers. Product p, requires 2 man - weeks of labour, while p, requires one man -
week of labour. Profit margin on p, is Rs. 60 and on p, is Rs. 40. Formulate it as linear
programming problem. Find solution graphically.

A sample of 100 students is taken from a large population. The mean height of the students nt G)
in this sample is 160cm. Can it be reasonable regarded that, in the population, the mean heig
is 165cm, and the standard deviation is 10cm? Level of significance 1%.

b)

c) The first four moments of a distribution about 2 are 1, 2.5, 5.5 and 16. Calculate the four
moments about mean.
d) In a binomial distribution consisting of 5 independent trials, probability of 1 and 2
successes are 0.4096 and 0.2048 respectively. Find the parameter p of the distribution.

b) The effectiveness of solar-energy heating units depends on the amount of radiation
available from the sun. During a typical October, daily total solar radiation in Tampa,

Que.4)

a) A hospital specialized in heart surgery and other allied treatments. During the previous
year, 1800 patients were admitted for treatment and the average payment made by a patient
was Rs. 120000 with standard deviation of Rs 25000. Find:

i) The number of patients who paid between Rs 100000 to Rs 150000; ©)
ii) The probability that a patient’s bill exceeds Rs 175000;
iii) The maximum amount paid by the lowest paying one-third patients.

b) An insurance salesman sells policies to 5 men of identical age and good health. According
to the actuarial tables, the probability that a man of this particular age will be alive 30 years
hence is 2/3. Find the probability that 30 years hence: (4)

i) at least one man will be alive; ii) at least 3 men will be alive.

c) A manufacturer, who produces medicine bottles, finds that 0.1% of the bottles are
defective. The bottles are packed in boxes containing 500 bottles. A drug manufacturer
buys 100 boxes from the producer of bottles. Using Poisson distribution find out how many 4)
boxes will contain: i) no defective; ii) at least 2 defectives.

Que.5)

a) A random selection of home loan amount sanctioned by a private sector bank and a

nationalized bank to different customers in India are shown below:
Private sector bank (Amount sanctioned in lakhs) | 35 | 42 | 29 | 18 | 21
Nationalized bank (Amount sanctioned in lakhs) | 27 | 35 | 26 | 37 | 30 ch)

al Test whether the average amount of home loan sanctioned by the nationalized bank is
higher at 5% level of significance.

A continuous random variable X has the following probability density function

f(x) =k’ e*, x20. Find k, mean and variance.

Seven coins are tossed and the number of heads obtained is noted. The experiment is repeated
128 times and the following distribution is obtained:

Number |0 1 2 3 4 5 6 7 Total
of heads
frequency | 7 6 19 35 30 23 7 1-- 128

Fit a binomial distribution if the coins are unbiased.

A manufacturer of electric bulbs sends out 500 lots, each consisting of 100 bulbs. If 5% bulbs
are defective, in how many lots can we expect
i) 97 or more good bulbs, ii) less than 97 good bulbs.

The weights of 4000 students are found to be normally distributed with mean 50 kgs, and
standard deviation 5 kgs. Find the probability that a student selected at random will have
weight i) less than 45 kgs ii) between 45 and 60 kgs.

A machinist is expected to make engine parts with axle diameter of 1.75 cm. A random sample
of 10 parts shows a mean diameter 1.85 cm with a standard deviation of 0.1 cm. On the basis
of this sample, can it be assumed that the work of the machinist is inferior at 5% level of
significance.

were obtained:

at 5% level of significance?

An electric bulb manufacturing company produces bulbs having a life time which is normally
distributed with mean 800 hours and standard deviation 40 hours. Find

i) the probability that a bulb selected at random will have life of more than 834 hours.

ii) the probability that bulb lasts between 778 and 834 hours.

An irregular 6-faced dice is such that the probability that it gives 3 even numbers in 5 throws is
twice the probability that it gives 2 even numbers in 5 throws. How many sets of exactly 5 trials
can be expected to give no even number out of 2500 sets?

production is 1.5. Over a period of one year the demand per day is distributed as Poisson
distribution. He employs two mechanics. On how many days in one year a) both mechanics
would be free, b) some demand is refused.

Q.4 (a) Assuming that half the population is vegetarian, so that the chance of an (4)
individual being vegetarian is 5 and assuming that 100 investigators can take

a sample of 10 individuals to see whether they are vegetarians. How many
investigators would you expect to report that three people or less were
vegetarians.

more than $34 hours (ii) the probability that bulb lasts between 778 and 834

hours.

A continuous random variable X has the probability density function f(x) =
0; elsewhere

Find & , mean and variance.

The probability that at any moment one telephone line out of 10 will be busy is 0.2.

i) What is the probability that 5 lines are busy?

ii) Find the expected number of busy lines and also find the probability of this number.
iii) What is the probability that all lines are busy?

The number of accidents in a year attributed to taxi drivers in a city follows Poisson distribution
with mean 3. Out of 1,000 taxi drivers, find approximately the number of drivers with (i) no
accident in a year, (ii) more than 3 accidents in a year.

The incomes of a group of 10,000 persons were found to be normally distributed with mean Rs. 520
and standard deviation Rs. 60. Find (i) the number of persons having incomes between Rs. 400 and
Rs. 550, (ii) the lowest income of the richest 500 persons.

Weights in kgs of 10 students are given below:

38,40,45,53,47,43,55,48,52,49

Can we say that variance of normal distribution from which the above sample is drawn is 20 kg?
Level of significance = 5%

Two sources of raw materials are under consideration of a company. Both sources seem to have
similar characteristics, but the company is not sure about their respective uniformity. A sample of
10 lot from source A yields variance of 225 and a sample of 11 lot from source B yields a variance
of 200. Is it likely to conclude that the variance of source A is significantly greater than the variance
of source B? Level of significance = 5%.

| Sample 1: | 19 | 17 | 15 | 21 | 16 | 18 | 16 | 14

@) The probability that a bomb dropped from a plane will strike the target is : . If six

bombs are dropped, find the probability that (i) exactly two bombs will hit the target
(ii) at least two will hit the target.

(b) Acar hire firm has two cars which it hires out day by day. The number of demands fora (4)
car on each day is distributed as Poisson distribution with mean 1.5. Calculate the
proportion of days on which i) neither car is used, ii) some demand is refused.

(c) The marks obtained by students in a college are normally distributed with mean 65 and (4)
variance 25. If 3 students are selected at random from this college what is the probability
that at least one of them would have scored more than 75 marks?

QS.

(a) The mean breaking strength of the cables supplied by a manufacturer is 1800, with an (4)
standard deviation of 100. By a new technique in the manufacturing process, it is claimed
that the breaking strength of the cable has increased. To test this claim, a sample of 50
cables is tested and it is found that the mean breaking strength is 1850. Can we support
the claim at 1% level of signification?

(b) A machinist is expected to make engine parts with axle diameter of 1.75 cm. A random (4)
sample of 10 parts shows a mean diameter of 1.85 cm, with an standard deviation of 0.1
cm. On the basis of this sample, would you say that the work of the machinist is inferior?

(c) Asample of size 13 gave an estimated population variance of 3.0, while another sample (4)
of size 15 gave an estimate of 2.5. Could both samples be from populations with the same
variance?

Que.1)
a) Senior Secondary Education Board says that the average marks obtained by students in
the final examination are 240 with a standard deviation of 37 marks. But principals of
public schools affiliated with the board say that their students have scored better. A
sample of 358 public school students was taken and the average of their marks was
found to be 246. Can the Board accept the statement made by principals of public
schools at 0.01 LOS?

more than 27. Find the standard deviation of X.

A just look at a bulb and tell whether or not it is defective. Find the probability distribution (3)

is much more that such a proportion in case of commercial cars. Hence, there is an
urgent need to increase the annual insurance premium in case of private cars. To check
the hypothesis of the insurance company, a committee of some enlightened citizens was. 7
formed and it conducted a random study. Out of random sample of 200 private cars 6)
under insurance 12 reported claim of theft. On the other hand claims of*only 4

will pass the examination?

A sample of 100 dry battery cells tested and found that average
life of 12 hours and standard deviation 3 hours. Assuming the

days in a year on which some demand is refused.

Que.4)

a) The ratio of probabilities of 3 successes in 5 independent trials to the
probability of 2 successes in 5 independent trials is %. What is the probability
of 4 successes in 6 independent trials?

b) A car hire firm has two cars which it hires out day by day. The number of
demands for a car on each day is distributed as Poisson variate with mean 1.5.

Calculate the proportion of days on which, i. Neither car is used, ii. Some
demands are refused.

c) The average monthly sales of 5,000 firms is normally distributed. Its mean
and standard deviation are Rs. 36,000 and Rs 10,000 respectively. Find i. the
number of firms, the sales of which are over Rs. 40,000. ii. Percentage of
firms the sales of which lie between Rs. 38,500 and Rs. 41,000. iii. The

number of firms the sales of which will lie between Rs. 30,000 and Rs.
40,000.

a) A simple random sample survey in respect of monthly earnings of semi-
skilled workers in two cities gives the following statistical information:

City Mean Earnings(Rs) Std Deviation(Rs) Sample Size

A 695 40 200 (6)

difference between monthly earnings of workers in two cities.

b) The following data indicates the biological values of protein from cow’s milk
and buffalo’s milk at a certain level:

as Cow’s Milk 1.82 | 2.02 | 1.88 | 1.61 | 1.81 1.54 ] 6

at 5% Level of Significance.
c) Genetic theory states that children having one parent of blood type A and one
parent of blood type B will always be of one of the three types A, AB and B
and the proportion of these three types will on an average be-as 1:2:1. The (8)

report states that out of 300 children having one A parent and one B parent,
30% were of type A, 45% were of type AB and remaining were of type B.
Test the hypothesis using Chi-Square ( y”) Test.

3 hours of hand operations and 8 hours of machine operations and each of the
articles B requires 4 hours of hand operations and 10 hours of machine
operations. Only 60 hours of hand operations and 100 hours of machine
operations are available per week. The Profit on each article of A is Rs. 5 and
on each article of B is Rs. 6. Supposing that the firm can produce as many

articles as it can, formulate the Linear Programming problem and solve using (10)
graphical method.
